% at_commands.tex - the AT command set of D-Modem's modem core
% (core/at.cpp), as dmodemd, libdmodem and the PBX modules have it.
% Built with docs/at/build.sh into docs/at_commands.pdf, on A5 pages
% (half an A4 sheet), and docs/at_commands-booklet.pdf, the same pages
% two to an A4 sheet in booklet order (at_commands_booklet.tex).
\documentclass[a5paper,10pt]{article}

\usepackage[T1]{fontenc}
\usepackage[utf8]{inputenc}
\usepackage{lmodern}                    % Latin Modern Mono for commands
\usepackage[scaled=0.92]{helvet}        % Helvetica for the text
\renewcommand{\familydefault}{\sfdefault}
\usepackage{microtype}
\setlength{\emergencystretch}{3em}
% no words broken with a hyphen at the end of a line
\hyphenpenalty=10000
\uchyph=0
% no paragraph leaves a single line alone at the foot or the head of a page
\clubpenalty=10000
\widowpenalty=10000
\displaywidowpenalty=10000
% A5: 148 x 210 mm, a text block of 11.8 x 17.1 cm
\usepackage[a5paper,left=1.5cm,right=1.5cm,top=2.1cm,bottom=1.8cm,
  headheight=12pt,headsep=0.45cm,footskip=0.8cm]{geometry}
\usepackage[table]{xcolor}
\usepackage{booktabs}
\usepackage{longtable}
\usepackage{array}
\usepackage{enumitem}
\usepackage{needspace}
\usepackage{pdfrender}
\usepackage{etoolbox}
\usepackage{fancyhdr}
\usepackage{lastpage}
\usepackage{titlesec}
\usepackage{tocloft}
\usepackage{tikz}
\usepackage[most]{tcolorbox}
\usepackage[colorlinks=true,linkcolor=dmlink,urlcolor=dmlink,
  bookmarksnumbered=true,bookmarksopen=true,bookmarksopenlevel=1,
  pdftitle={D-Modem AT Command Reference},
  pdfauthor={D-Modem},
  pdfsubject={The AT commands of dmodemd, libdmodem and the PBX modules},
  pdfkeywords={AT commands, modem, V.250, S-registers}]{hyperref}
\usepackage{bookmark}

% the document's name and edition, for the title page and the footer
\newcommand{\docname}{D-Modem AT Command Reference}
% the software version (CMakeLists.txt's) and the date (the last commit
% to this file's), written by build.sh
\InputIfFileExists{dmversion.tex}{}{%
  \newcommand{\dmversion}{1.0.0}\newcommand{\dmdate}{\today}}
\newcommand{\docedition}{\dmdate}

% colours, D-Modem's (the brand repository's palette, its Red Book for
% this repository): the navy of the band and the headings, the red of
% the accents, a darker red for red text, the tints of the command
% strips and of the tables, the links, the ink of the mark's carrier
% and grey for the footer
\definecolor{dmnavy}{HTML}{0A0F1C}
\definecolor{dmred}{HTML}{FF4D5E}
\definecolor{dmredtext}{HTML}{C8283A}
\definecolor{dmtint}{HTML}{FFF0F2}
\definecolor{dmhead}{HTML}{FFDDE1}
\definecolor{dmlink}{HTML}{C8283A}
\definecolor{dmink}{HTML}{E8F0FF}
\definecolor{dmmuted}{HTML}{A9B4D0}
\definecolor{dmgrey}{HTML}{6B7591}
\definecolor{dmrc}{HTML}{E4E9F3}

% the D-Modem mark (brand/tools/gen.py): a D, the carrier inside it
% a sine turning to square pulses; the SVG's units, y down
\newcommand{\dmlogo}[1]{\begin{tikzpicture}[x=#1/500,y=-#1/500]
  \foreach \w/\o in {70/0.10,54/0.18}
    \draw[dmred,line width=\w*#1/500,opacity=\o,line join=round]
      (140,100) -- (240,100) arc[start angle=-90,end angle=90,radius=150] -- (140,400) -- cycle;
  \draw[dmred,line width=38*#1/500,line join=round]
    (140,100) -- (240,100) arc[start angle=-90,end angle=90,radius=150] -- (140,400) -- cycle;
  \draw[dmink,line width=16*#1/500,line cap=round,line join=round]
    (178.0,250.0) -- (181.1,235.5) -- (184.1,222.0) -- (187.2,210.4) --
    (190.3,201.5) -- (193.3,195.9) -- (196.4,194.0) -- (199.5,195.9) --
    (202.5,201.5) -- (205.6,210.4) -- (208.7,222.0) -- (211.7,235.5) --
    (214.8,250.0) -- (217.9,264.5) -- (220.9,278.0) -- (224.0,289.6) --
    (227.1,298.5) -- (230.1,304.1) -- (233.2,306.0) -- (236.3,304.1) --
    (239.3,298.5) -- (242.4,289.6) -- (245.5,278.0) -- (248.5,264.5) --
    (251.6,250.0) -- (254.7,235.5) -- (257.7,222.0) -- (260.8,210.4) --
    (263.9,201.5) -- (266.9,195.9) -- (270.0,194.0) -- (288.4,194.0) --
    (288.4,306.0) -- (325.2,306.0) -- (325.2,194.0) -- (362.0,194.0);
\end{tikzpicture}}

\setlist{nosep,leftmargin=*}

% examples of what the modem says: small enough for 72 columns (AT&V's)
% across the A5 page
\AtBeginEnvironment{verbatim}{\footnotesize}
% and on a tint of their own, so that they stand out from the text
\definecolor{dmcode}{HTML}{F1F3F8}
% and \example{label} before each one names it, in a title bar
\definecolor{dmcodehead}{HTML}{DDE2EE}
\newcommand{\dmextitle}{Example}
\newcommand{\example}[1]{\renewcommand{\dmextitle}{#1}}
\tcolorboxenvironment{verbatim}{enhanced,breakable,colback=dmcode,
  colframe=dmcode,colbacktitle=dmcodehead,coltitle=dmnavy,
  fonttitle=\footnotesize\bfseries,title={\dmextitle},
  boxrule=0pt,arc=2pt,left=4pt,right=4pt,top=3pt,
  bottom=3pt,toptitle=1pt,bottomtitle=1pt,before skip=6pt,after skip=6pt}
\setlength{\parindent}{0pt}
\setlength{\parskip}{0.6ex}

% tables: tinted header rows, no gaps between the rules and the tint
\setlength{\aboverulesep}{0pt}
\setlength{\belowrulesep}{0pt}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.25}
\arrayrulecolor{dmnavy}
% a header row's cell
\newcommand{\thd}[1]{\normalfont\textbf{\color{dmnavy}#1}}

% a command or what the modem says, as typed
\newcommand{\cmd}[1]{\texttt{#1}}
% what the modem sends: result codes and responses, in type on a tint
\DeclareRobustCommand{\rc}[1]{{\setlength{\fboxsep}{1pt}%
  \colorbox{dmrc}{\ttfamily\color{dmnavy}\rule[-0.45ex]{0pt}{1.95ex}#1}}}
% a command not linked to an entry, in the links' red
\newcommand{\cmdr}[1]{\textcolor{dmlink}{\cmd{#1}}}
% a standard (V.34, T.30) or one of its signals (DIS, PREP), in type
\newcommand{\std}[1]{\texttt{#1}}
% the same in a bold heading: Latin Modern Mono has no true bold, so
% its letters are stroked as well as filled
\newcommand{\stdb}[1]{\textpdfrender{TextRenderingMode=FillStroke,
  LineWidth=0.25pt}{\texttt{#1}}}
% the backslash, as in AT\N
\newcommand{\bs}{\textbackslash}
% a parameter
\newcommand{\p}[1]{\textmd{\textit{#1}}}
% a command, linked to its entry (key: the entry's, see command below)
\newcommand{\cmdl}[2][]{\hyperlink{cmd:#1}{\cmd{#2}}}
% S-register n, linked to its definition
\newcommand{\sreg}[1]{\hyperlink{sreg:#1}{S#1}}

% the bookmarks' text: the commands as they are typed
\makeatletter
\pdfstringdefDisableCommands{%
  \let\cmd\@firstofone
  \let\p\@firstofone
  \def\bs{\textbackslash}%
}
\makeatother

% the manual's template, after the modem manuals of its day (Lucent's
% Venus reference among them): each command a strip with its name and
% title, a few sentences, its syntax and result codes as bullets, and a
% numbered table of its values; each S-register its title, then its
% range, default and units

% list bullets: small squares in the band's colour
\renewcommand{\labelitemi}{\color{dmred}\rule[0.3ex]{0.55ex}{0.55ex}}

% Entries kept whole: an entry (a command or an S-register) that would
% run on to the next page starts there instead, unless it is longer
% than a page.  Each entry's height is measured (\pdfsavepos) and saved
% in the .aux; the next run needs that much room before the entry, and
% a heading that its first entry follows needs room for both.
\makeatletter
\newcount\dm@entry
\newcount\dm@head  % headings so far
\newif\ifdm@open
\newif\ifdm@whole  % the entry has room to be whole: no breaks inside
\newdimen\dm@stripskip \dm@stripskip=26pt % a strip's skip and height
\newdimen\dm@ybot \dm@ybot=1.8cm   % the text block's bottom (geometry)
% from the .aux: \dm@pos{s or e}{entry}{y}{page}, \dm@att{entry}{heading}
\def\dm@pos#1#2#3#4{\expandafter\gdef\csname dm@#1@#2\endcsname{{#3}{#4}}}
\def\dm@att#1#2{\expandafter\gdef\csname dm@att@#1\endcsname{#2}}
\def\dm@mark#1{\pdfsavepos\edef\dm@tmp{\noexpand\write\noexpand\@auxout
  {\string\dm@pos{#1}{\the\dm@entry}{\noexpand\the\pdflastypos}%
   {\noexpand\the\c@page}}}\dm@tmp}
% \dm@height{n}: entry n's height last run into \dimen@ (0pt if unknown)
\def\dm@height#1{\dimen@\z@
  \expandafter\ifx\csname dm@s@#1\endcsname\relax\else
  \expandafter\ifx\csname dm@e@#1\endcsname\relax\else
    \edef\dm@tmp{\csname dm@s@#1\endcsname\csname dm@e@#1\endcsname}%
    \expandafter\dm@calc\dm@tmp\fi\fi}
\def\dm@calc#1#2#3#4{% start y, page; end y, page
  \ifnum#2=#4\relax \dimen@=\dimexpr#1sp-#3sp\relax
  \else \dimen@=\dimexpr#1sp-\dm@ybot+\textheight*(#4-#2-1)
    +\dm@ybot+\textheight-#3sp\relax\fi}
% room for \dimen@ plus #1, if the entry fits on a page
\def\dm@need#1{\global\dm@wholefalse
  \ifdim\dimen@>\z@\ifdim\dimen@<\dimexpr\textheight-#1-2pt\relax
  \needspace{\dimexpr\dimen@+#1+2pt\relax}\global\dm@wholetrue\fi\fi}
% close the open entry; start entry n+1 (#1 its usual \needspace, #2
% the room its strip or heading takes above the measured start)
\def\dm@close{\ifdm@open\par\dm@mark{e}\global\dm@openfalse\fi}
\def\dm@start#1#2{\dm@close\global\advance\dm@entry\@ne
  \if@nobreak\immediate\write\@auxout
    {\string\dm@att{\the\dm@entry}{\the\dm@head}}%
    \expandafter\ifx\csname dm@att@\the\dm@entry\endcsname\relax
    \global\dm@wholefalse\fi
  \else\dm@height{\the\dm@entry}\dm@need{#2}\needspace{#1}\fi
  \global\dm@opentrue}
% before a heading: room for it and the entry that follows it, if one
% follows it straight away
\def\dm@ahead#1{\dm@close\global\dm@wholefalse\global\advance\dm@head\@ne
  \expandafter\ifx\csname dm@att@\the\numexpr\dm@entry+1\endcsname\relax
  \else\ifnum\csname dm@att@\the\numexpr\dm@entry+1\endcsname=\dm@head
  \dm@height{\the\numexpr\dm@entry+1}\dm@need{#1+\dm@stripskip}\fi\fi}
\AtBeginDocument{%
  \pretocmd{\section}{\dm@ahead{5\baselineskip}}{}{\dm@patchfailed}%
  \pretocmd{\subsection}{\dm@ahead{3\baselineskip}}{}{\dm@patchfailed}}
\makeatother

% one command: "name---title" in a tinted strip (a link target and a
% bookmark under its section, as cmd:<key>), then what it does; on the
% next page unless the strip and nine lines of its text fit (straight after a
% heading, the heading keeps them with it)
\newtcolorbox{cmdstrip}{enhanced,colback=dmtint,colframe=dmred,
  boxrule=0pt,leftrule=3pt,arc=0pt,outer arc=0pt,left=6pt,right=6pt,
  top=3pt,bottom=3pt,before skip=11pt,
  after={\par\nobreak\nointerlineskip\vspace{\glueexpr 4pt-\parskip}}}
\makeatletter
% \begin{command}{key}{name}{title}
\newenvironment{command}[3]
  {\par\dm@start{9\baselineskip}{\dm@stripskip}%
   \begin{cmdstrip}\hypertarget{cmd:#1}{}\dm@mark{s}%
     \bookmark[dest={cmd:#1},rellevel=1,keeplevel]{#2 - #3}%
     \textbf{\color{dmredtext}\cmd{#2}\,---\,#3}\end{cmdstrip}\nopagebreak
   \begin{list}{}{\leftmargin=0.8em\topsep=0pt\@beginparpenalty=\@M}%
   \item[]}
  {\par\dm@mark{e}\global\dm@openfalse\end{list}}
\makeatother

% "Syntax:" and the forms the command takes, one a bullet
\makeatletter
\newcommand{\dmneedinside}[1]{\ifdm@whole\else\needspace{#1}\fi}
\makeatother
\newenvironment{syntax}{\par\dmneedinside{3\baselineskip}Syntax:%
  \begin{itemize}\ttfamily\raggedright}{\end{itemize}}
% the result codes, a small table (not numbered) of each code and when
% it comes, a \res{code}{when} for each
\newenvironment{results}{\par\dmneedinside{4\baselineskip}%
  \vspace{2pt}\small\begin{tabular}{|>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.25\textwidth}
    |>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.62\textwidth}|}
  \hline\rowcolor{dmhead}\thd{Result code} & \thd{When} \\ \hline}
  {\end{tabular}\par\vspace{4pt}}
\newcommand{\res}[2]{#1 & #2 \\ \hline}
% an S-register's range, default and units, a bullet each
\newcommand{\facts}[3]{\begin{itemize}
  \item Range: #1 \item \emph{Default}: #2 \item Units: #3\end{itemize}}

% table columns: centred commands, ragged text, ragged commands
\newcolumntype{C}[1]{>{\centering\arraybackslash\ttfamily}p{#1}}
\newcolumntype{L}[1]{>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{#1}}
\newcolumntype{T}[1]{>{\raggedright\arraybackslash\ttfamily}p{#1}}
\newcolumntype{R}[1]{>{\raggedleft\arraybackslash}p{#1}}

% a numbered table, ruled as a grid: \begin{vtable}{columns}{column
% spec}{caption}{header row}, then rows each ending \\ \hline; its
% caption again, "(continued)", on each page it runs on to
\makeatletter
\newcounter{dmtable}
\newcommand{\tabcap}[1]{\textbf{\color{dmredtext}Table~#1.}}
\newenvironment{vtable}[4]
  {\par\addvspace{6pt}\refstepcounter{dmtable}\small
   \setlength{\LTleft}{0pt}\setlength{\LTpre}{4pt}%
   \setlength{\LTpost}{10pt}%
   \begin{longtable}{#2}
   \multicolumn{#1}{@{}l}{\normalfont\tabcap{\thedmtable}\ #3}\\[1pt]
   \hline\rowcolor{dmhead}#4\\ \hline
   \endfirsthead
   \multicolumn{#1}{@{}l}{\normalfont\tabcap{\thedmtable}\
     {\let\label\@gobble #3} \textit{(continued)}}\\[1pt]
   \hline\rowcolor{dmhead}#4\\ \hline
   \endhead}
  {\end{longtable}}
\makeatother

% an S-register's definition: S<n>---<title> (a link target, as
% sreg:<n>, and a bookmark), then what it does and its \facts
\makeatletter
\newcommand{\sregdef}[2]{\par\dm@close\addvspace{8pt}\dm@start{6\baselineskip}{14pt}%
  \hypertarget{sreg:#1}{}\dm@mark{s}%
  \bookmark[dest={sreg:#1},rellevel=1,keeplevel]{S#1 - #2}%
  {\bfseries\color{dmredtext}S#1---#2}\par\nobreak\@afterheading}
\makeatother

% headings: the section's number in a box of the band's colour
\titleformat{\section}{\large\bfseries\color{dmnavy}}
  {\sbox0{\thesection}\colorbox{dmred}{\makebox[\ifdim\wd0>1.1em\wd0\else1.1em\fi]{\color{white}\thesection}}}{0.6em}{}
  [{\color{dmred}\titlerule[1.2pt]}]
\titlespacing*{\section}{0pt}{3.2ex plus 1ex minus .2ex}{1.6ex}
% each section on a page of its own
\newcommand{\sectionbreak}{\clearpage}
% each subsection also starts on a new page (titlesec adds no break
% straight after a section heading)
\newcommand{\subsectionbreak}{\clearpage}
\titleformat{\subsection}{\normalsize\bfseries\color{dmredtext}}
  {\textcolor{dmred}{\thesubsection}}{0.6em}{}
\titleformat{\paragraph}[runin]{\bfseries\color{dmnavy}}{}{0pt}{}

% the contents: sections in the band's colour, subsections dotted
\renewcommand{\cftsecfont}{\bfseries\color{dmnavy}}
\renewcommand{\cftsecpagefont}{\bfseries\color{dmnavy}}
\renewcommand{\cfttoctitlefont}{\large\bfseries\color{dmnavy}}
\setlength{\cftbeforesecskip}{0.45ex}
% every entry dotted to its page number, the dots grey and close together
\renewcommand{\cftsecleader}{{\color{dmmuted}\cftdotfill{\cftdotsep}}}
\renewcommand{\cftsubsecleader}{{\color{dmmuted}\cftdotfill{\cftdotsep}}}
\renewcommand{\cftdotsep}{1.5}
\renewcommand{\cftmarktoc}{\markboth{\contentsname}{\contentsname}}

% every page: a band across the top with the document's name and the
% section, an accent stripe under it; the footer the name, the edition
% and the page of how many
% a square QAM constellation: #1 points a side, #2 apart, centred at
% #3, drawn in #4 (with its I and Q axes when #5 is not empty); each
% \qamstep rings out from the centre the points are half the size
\newcommand{\qamstep}{1}
\newcommand{\qam}[5]{%
  \begin{scope}[shift={#3}]
    \pgfmathsetmacro{\qamh}{(#1-1)/2}
    \if\relax\detokenize{#5}\relax\else
      \draw[#4,line width=0.4pt] ({-(\qamh+0.9)*#2},0) -- ({(\qamh+0.9)*#2},0);
      \draw[#4,line width=0.4pt] (0,{-(\qamh+0.9)*#2}) -- (0,{(\qamh+0.9)*#2});
    \fi
    \foreach \i in {1,...,#1}
      \foreach \q in {1,...,#1}
        {\pgfmathsetmacro{\qamk}{max(abs(\i-1-\qamh),abs(\q-1-\qamh))-0.5}
         \fill[#4] ({(\i-1-\qamh)*#2},{(\q-1-\qamh)*#2})
           circle[radius={0.34*#2/pow(2,\qamk/\qamstep)}];}
  \end{scope}}
\renewcommand{\sectionmark}[1]{\markboth{\thesection\quad #1}{}}
\newcommand{\pageband}{%
  \begin{tikzpicture}[remember picture,overlay]
    \fill[dmnavy] (current page.north west) rectangle
      ([yshift=-1.25cm]current page.north east);
    \fill[dmred] ([yshift=-1.25cm]current page.north west) rectangle
      ([yshift=-1.37cm]current page.north east);
    \begin{scope}[shift={(current page.north)}]
      \foreach \x in {0,...,29}
        \foreach \y in {1,...,4}
          \fill[dmmuted,opacity=0.3] ({0.125cm+\x*0.25cm},{-\y*0.25cm}) circle[radius=0.025cm];
    \end{scope}
    \node[anchor=base west,text=white,inner sep=0pt]
      at ([xshift=1.5cm,yshift=-0.75cm]current page.north west)
      {\small\bfseries D-Modem\quad\mdseries AT Command Reference};
    \node[anchor=base east,text=white,inner sep=0pt]
      at ([xshift=-1.5cm,yshift=-0.75cm]current page.north east)
      {\footnotesize\leftmark};
  \end{tikzpicture}}
\newif\ifdmmain
% the footer: the page number on the outer edge (right on an odd page,
% left on an even one), the name and edition on the inner
\newcommand{\dmfootname}{\docname\ \textbullet\ \docedition}
\newcommand{\dmfootpage}{Page \thepage\ifdmmain\ of \pageref*{dm:last}\fi}
\fancypagestyle{dm}{%
  \fancyhf{}%
  \fancyhead[L]{\pageband}%
  \fancyfoot[L]{\footnotesize\color{dmgrey}\ifodd\value{page}\dmfootname
    \else\dmfootpage\fi}%
  \fancyfoot[R]{\footnotesize\color{dmgrey}\ifodd\value{page}\dmfootpage
    \else\dmfootname\fi}%
  \renewcommand{\headrulewidth}{0pt}%
  \renewcommand{\footrulewidth}{0.5pt}%
  \renewcommand{\footrule}{{\color{dmhead}%
    \hrule width\headwidth height\footrulewidth \vskip\footruleskip}}}
\tocloftpagestyle{dm}
\pagestyle{dm}


% the end of the manual: ruled notes pages, so that the pages between
% the covers come to a multiple of 8 (A3 sheets folded twice), then the
% back cover
\newcommand{\notespage}{%
  \markboth{Notes}{}%
  {\large\bfseries\color{dmnavy}Notes}\par\vspace{1ex}%
  {\color{dmred}\hrule height 1.2pt}\vspace{2ex}%
  \noindent\begin{tikzpicture}
    \begin{scope}[overlay]\renewcommand{\qamstep}{2}%
     \ifodd\value{page}
       \qam{16}{0.6cm}{(\linewidth-50.4mm,-147mm+50.4mm)}{dmred,opacity=0.12}{axes}%
     \else
       \qam{16}{0.6cm}{(50.4mm,-147mm+50.4mm)}{dmred,opacity=0.12}{axes}%
     \fi\end{scope}
    \foreach \l in {0,...,21}
      \draw[dmhead,line width=0.5pt] (0.25pt,-\l*7mm) -- (\linewidth-0.25pt,-\l*7mm);
  \end{tikzpicture}\clearpage}
\makeatletter
\newcount\dm@front % the roman pages
\newcommand{\dm@notes}{%
  \edef\dm@tmp{\the\numexpr\dm@front+\value{page}-1\relax}%
  \ifnum\numexpr\dm@tmp-8*((\dm@tmp-4)/8)\relax=0
  \else\notespage\expandafter\dm@notes\fi}
\newcommand{\dmnotes}{\clearpage\pdfbookmark[1]{Notes}{notes}\dm@notes
  \immediate\write\@auxout{\string\newlabel{dm:last}%
    {{}{\the\numexpr\value{page}-1\relax}{}{}{}}}}
% the front matter (the contents and the first three sections, numbered
% in roman) and the main matter, from page 1 and section 1
\newcommand{\frontmatter}{\clearpage\pagenumbering{roman}%
  \renewcommand{\thesection}{\Roman{section}}%
  \renewcommand{\theHsection}{front.\arabic{section}}}
\newcommand{\mainmatter}{\clearpage\global\dm@front=\numexpr\value{page}-1\relax
  \pagenumbering{arabic}\global\dmmaintrue
  \setcounter{section}{0}%
  \renewcommand{\thesection}{\arabic{section}}%
  \renewcommand{\theHsection}{\arabic{section}}}
\makeatother
\newcommand{\backcover}{%
  \thispagestyle{empty}%
  \begin{tikzpicture}[remember picture,overlay]
    \fill[dmnavy] ([yshift=12cm]current page.south west) rectangle
      (current page.south east);
    \fill[dmred] ([yshift=12.3cm]current page.south west) rectangle
      ([yshift=12cm]current page.south east);
    \begin{scope}[shift={([yshift=7.4cm]current page.south west)}]
      \foreach \a/\f/\o in {0.8/1.8/0.10, 0.5/2.9/0.08, 1.1/1.0/0.06}
        \draw[white,opacity=\o,line width=1.2pt,domain=0:14.8,samples=300,
          smooth] plot (\x,{\a*sin(\f*\x r+2)});
    \end{scope}
    {\renewcommand{\qamstep}{2}%
     \qam{16}{0.52cm}{([xshift=4.2cm,yshift=6.9cm]current page.south west)}{white,opacity=0.13}{axes}}
    \node[anchor=north east,inner sep=0pt]
      at ([xshift=-1.5cm,yshift=10.9cm]current page.south east)
      {\dmlogo{1.8cm}};
    \node[anchor=south west,text=white,align=left,inner sep=0pt,
      text width=11.8cm]
      at ([xshift=1.5cm,yshift=1.6cm]current page.south west)
      {{\large\bfseries D-Modem}\quad{\color{dmmuted}AT Command Reference}\\[4pt]
       {\small\color{dmmuted}Version \dmversion\ \textbullet\ \dmdate}\\[10pt]
       {\small\color{dmmuted}\texttt{https://github.com/D-Modem/D-Modem}}\\[4pt]
       {\footnotesize\color{dmmuted}\dmccby{dmmuted}\quad Copyright
        \textcopyright\ the D-Modem Authors.  This document is licensed
        under CC BY 4.0.  The D-Modem software is available under the
        GNU AGPL, version~3 only, or a commercial licence.}};
  \end{tikzpicture}}
% the Creative Commons BY badge, drawn (no image file), in colour #1
% (inside a cover's node, a picture in it takes the node's anchor, text
% width and text colour: its nodes set their own)
\tikzset{dmccby/.style={#1,text=#1,anchor=center,text width={},
  align=none,inner sep=0pt}}
\newcommand{\dmccby}[1]{\tikz[baseline=-0.6ex]{%
  \draw[#1,line width=0.5pt,rounded corners=1.5pt] (0,-0.17) rectangle
    (0.9,0.17);

\section{Introduction}

This manual gives the \cmdr{AT} command set of the modem core of D-Modem
(\cmd{core/} in the source).  All D-Modem programs use this core.  The
core accepts the commands from these programs:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{\texttt{dmodemd}}: the modem on a pseudo terminal
  (\cmd{/dev/ttyDM0} is a link to it).  Its line is a \std{SIP} call.
  With \texttt{-a} and \texttt{--usb}, its line is an analogue telephone
  line.
\item \textbf{libdmodem} (\cmd{dmodem.h}): the modem as a library.  The
  program that contains the library sends the commands.
\item The \textbf{Asterisk and FreeSWITCH modules} (\cmd{app\_dmodem},
  \cmd{mod\_dmodem}).  The profiles of these modules give the commands
  that start each call.
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{Feature Overview}

\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Data} from \std{300}~bit/s to \std{56000}~bit/s: \std{V.21}, \std{V.22},
  \std{V.22bis}, \std{V.23}, Bell 103 and 212A, \std{V.32}, \std{V.32bis},
  \std{V.34} up to \std{33600}~bit/s, and \std{V.90} and \std{V.92} as the
  digital modem.  Automode finds the best modulation that the two modems
  have (\hyperref[sec:modulation]{Section~\ref*{sec:modulation}}).
\item \textbf{Error correction and compression}: \std{V.42} (\std{LAPM}) and
  \std{MNP} 2 to 5 for error correction.  \std{V.42bis}, \std{V.44}, \std{MNP~5}
  and zstd for compression
  (\hyperref[sec:errctl]{Sections~\ref*{sec:errctl}} and
  \hyperref[sec:compression]{\ref*{sec:compression}}).
\item \textbf{V.92's extras}: quick connect, \std{PCM} upstream and
  modem-on-hold.  With modem-on-hold, you can answer a waiting call and
  then return to the data call.
\item \textbf{Fax}: \std{Class 1} (\std{T.31}), \std{Class 2} and \std{Class 2.0} (\std{T.32}),
  up to \std{14400}~bit/s with \std{V.17}.  Adaptive answer finds if a call is
  a fax call or a data call (\hyperref[sec:fax]{Section~\ref*{sec:fax}}).
\item \textbf{Voice} (\std{V.253}): play and record audio, send and
  detect \std{DTMF} and tones, use a handset, and change a voice call into a
  fax call or a data call (\hyperref[sec:voice]{Section~\ref*{sec:voice}}).
\item \textbf{Text telephones} (\std{V.18}): the 5-bit \std{TTY} of the deaf
  community, telex and \std{RTTY}, \std{DTMF}, \std{EDT}, Bell 103, the \std{V.23}
  of Minitel, the \std{V.21} text telephones, the mode of \std{V.18}, and
  automode between these modes (\hyperref[sec:text]{Section~\ref*{sec:text}}).
\item \textbf{Caller ID} from a \std{SIP} call or from a telephone line (\std{FSK}
  and \std{DTMF}).  In voice mode, the caller ID of a waiting call.
\item \textbf{Leased lines}, 2-wire and 4-wire (\std{V.29}, \std{V.33},
  \std{V.27ter}), with the simulated carrier control of \std{V.13}.
\item \textbf{Digital channels}: \std{V.91} between two digital modems,
  the clear \std{64000}~bit/s channel of an \std{ISDN} data call (\std{X.75}, \std{PPP},
  \std{V.110}, \std{V.120}) (\hyperref[sec:isdn]{Section~\ref*{sec:isdn}}),
  and the synchronous access of \std{V.80}.
\item \textbf{V.cvm} for data on mobile speech paths, and
  \textbf{V.wb}, a wideband modem for a dry pair.
\item \textbf{Diagnostics}: the statistics of the last call (\cmdl[I]{ATI11},
  \cmdl[ampV]{AT\&V1}, \cmdl[hashUD]{AT\#UD}) and loop tests
  (\cmdl[ampT]{AT\&T}).
\item \textbf{The setup strings of other modems} work.  The modem accepts
  the commands of \std{V.250}, Rockwell and Conexant, and USRobotics modems.
  Most of these commands have no effect.  Their entries tell you this.
\end{itemize}

Each command, range, \emph{default} and response in this manual comes
from the core.  If a setting has no effect, its entry tells you.

\subsection*{How to Read This Manual}\label{sec:howto}
\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{\protect\numberline{}How to Read This Manual}
All command entries have the same structure:
\begin{itemize}
\item A strip with the name and the title of the command.
\item A description of the function of the command.
\item If applicable, the syntax, the result codes and a numbered table
  of the values.  The table marks the \emph{default} with \emph{(default)}.
\end{itemize}
\hyperref[tab:summary]{Table~\ref*{tab:summary}} lists all commands and
has a link to the entry of each command.  This manual shows the
commands in capital letters, but the modem accepts the two cases.
\p{n} is a decimal number and \p{h} is a hexadecimal number.
\verb|[ ]| shows an item that you do not have to type.  The
\emph{default} of a setting is the value that \cmdl[Z]{ATZ} sets.  The
program also sets this value when it starts.

The type style of the text shows what the text is:
\begin{itemize}
\item \cmdr{Red monospace} is a command or a part of a command, as you
  type it (\cmdl[D]{ATD}, \cmdl[plusMS]{+MS=V92}).  If it is a link, it
  goes to the entry of that command.
\item \rc{Monospace on blue} is what the modem sends back: a result
  code or a different response, as the screen of the \std{DTE} shows it
  (\rc{OK}, \rc{CONNECT 33600}, \rc{NO CARRIER}).
\item \std{Plain monospace} is a standard, a signal or a term from a
  standard (\std{V.34}, \std{CNG}), an acronym (\std{DTE}), or a
  number, for example a speed (\std{33600}~bit/s).
\item \p{Italics} show a value that you must supply (\cmdl[S]{ATS}\p{n}).
\end{itemize}

\section{Common Terms}\label{sec:terms}

This section gives the terms that this manual uses frequently.  The
glossary (\hyperref[sec:glossary]{Section~\ref*{sec:glossary}}) gives
the terms that are less frequent.

\begin{description}[style=nextline,leftmargin=1.2em,font=\bfseries\color{dmnavy}]
\item[Answer tone] The tone that a modem sends first when it answers a
  call: 2100~Hz (\std{ANS}).  \std{V.8} modems send \std{ANSam}: the same
  tone, with a slow change of its amplitude.  A calling modem that hears
  the answer tone starts its part of the handshake.
\item[\textpdfrender{TextRenderingMode=FillStroke,LineWidth=0.25pt}{\cmdr{AT}} command] \cmdr{AT} is short for ``attention''.  An \cmdr{AT}
  command is an instruction to the modem.  It is text that starts with
  the letters \cmdr{AT}, for example \cmdl[Z]{ATZ}.  One line can
  contain more than one command (\cmdr{ATE1Q0V1}).
\item[Basic and extended commands] A basic command is a letter and a
  number (\cmdl[E]{E1}, \cmdl[ampK]{\&K3}).  Some basic commands have
  \cmdr{\&}, \cmdr{\bs} or \cmdr{\%} before the letter.  Extended
  commands start with \cmdr{+} or \cmdr{-} and accept values
  (\cmdl[plusMS]{+MS=V92}).
\item[Baud] Symbols per second: the number of times in one second that
  the modem changes its signal.  Each symbol can carry more than one
  bit.  Thus, the bit/s of a connection is frequently higher than its
  baud: \std{V.34} sends up to \std{3429} baud but \std{33600}~bit/s.
  The sellers of old modems gave the speed in baud.  For this reason,
  people frequently confuse the two words.
\item[bit/s] Bits per second, the speed of a connection.  \rc{CONNECT}
  gives this speed.
\item[Caller ID] The number of the caller, and sometimes the name, the
  date and the time.  The telephone network sends this data on the line.
  On many lines, it comes between the first ring and the second ring.
  The modem gives it with \cmdl[hashCID]{\#CID}.
\item[Carrier] The signal that the far end sends during a call.  If
  the modem loses the carrier, the call ends (\rc{NO CARRIER}).
\item[Command echo and line echo] Two different echoes.  The command echo
  is the characters that the modem sends back to the \std{DTE} in command
  mode (\cmdl[E]{E1}).  The line echo is a part of the signal of the modem
  that comes back from the telephone line.  The near echo comes from the
  local line, and the far echo comes from the far end of the connection.
  The echo canceller of the modem removes the line echo from the received
  signal.
\item[Command mode and online mode] In command mode, the modem reads
  the data from the \std{DTE} as commands.  When a call is connected
  (\rc{CONNECT}), the modem is online: the data from the \std{DTE} goes
  to the far end.  The escape sequence (\cmdr{+++}, with a pause before
  it and after it) returns the modem to command mode and keeps the call.
  \cmdl[O]{ATO} returns the modem to online mode
  (\hyperref[sec:online]{Section~\ref*{sec:online}}).
\item[\stdb{DCE}] Data Circuit-terminating Equipment.  The modem: the
  equipment between the \std{DTE} and the telephone line or call.  Here,
  the \std{DCE} is D-Modem.
\item[Default] The value of a setting after \cmdl[Z]{ATZ} or when the
  program starts.  The modem has no stored profiles.
\item[Dial string] The text after \cmdl[D]{ATD}: the number and the
  modifiers that change how the modem dials it (\cmdl[D]{T},
  \cmdl[D]{P}, \cmdl[D]{W}, \cmdl[D]{,} and other modifiers).
\item[Dial tone, busy tone and cadence] Tones of the telephone network.  The
  dial tone shows that the line is ready for a number.  The busy tone
  shows that the called line is in use.  The cadence of a tone is its
  pattern of tone and silence, for example 0.5~s on and 0.5~s off.  The
  modem waits for a dial tone before it dials (\sreg{6}).
  \cmdl[X]{X} sets which tones the modem identifies.
\item[Dry pair] A pair of copper wires with no other equipment on it:
  no telephone exchange, no battery voltage and no dial tone.  An
  example is two modems with a direct wire connection.  A modem uses a
  dry pair as a leased line.  \std{V.wb} operates only on a dry pair.
\item[\stdb{DTE}] Data Terminal Equipment.  The computer or program that
  communicates with the modem: a terminal program, a fax program,
  \texttt{pppd}, or the program that contains libdmodem.
\item[\stdb{DTMF}] Dual Tone Multi-Frequency.  The tones of a telephone
  keypad: two tones at the same time for each key.  The modem dials with
  these tones (\cmdl[D]{T}).  It also detects them in voice mode and in
  \std{DTMF} caller ID.
\item[Error correction and compression] Error correction (\std{V.42},
  \std{MNP}) sends again the data that the line damaged.  Compression
  (\std{V.42bis}, \std{V.44}) makes the data smaller, so that it goes
  faster.  The far end must also have these functions.
\item[Far end] The equipment at the other end of the call: the modem,
  the fax machine or the text telephone that this modem connects to.
  This manual uses ``far end'' for all of them.
\item[Fax] Facsimile.  A procedure that sends a page as an image through
  a telephone line.  The fax machine scans the page and its modem sends
  the image to a different fax machine.  That fax machine prints the
  page.  This modem can operate as the modem of a fax machine for a fax
  program on the \std{DTE} (\hyperref[sec:fax]{Section~\ref*{sec:fax}}).
\item[Flow control] The method that stops the flow of data when a buffer
  is full, and starts it again.  The \std{DTE} and the modem use the
  \std{RTS} and \std{CTS} signals (hardware flow control) or the
  \std{XON} and \std{XOFF} characters (software flow control).
  \cmdl[plusIFC]{+IFC} sets it.  On a pseudo terminal, the operating
  system controls the flow.
\item[Frame] A block of data with a start, an end and a checksum.  An
  \std{HDLC} frame starts and ends with a flag (\cmd{01111110}).  If the
  checksum is not correct, the receiver does not use the frame.  Then
  the error correction sends the frame again.
\item[Handshake] The signals that two modems send to each other at the
  start of a call.  With these signals, the modems select the
  modulation, the rate, the error correction and the compression.
  \cmdl[D]{ATD} and \cmdl[A]{ATA} start the handshake.  If the handshake
  fails, the result is \rc{NO CARRIER}.
\item[\stdb{ITU}] International Telecommunication Union.  The agency of
  the United Nations for telecommunications.  Its standards sector,
  ITU-T, writes the recommendations that modems obey: the V.\ldots{}
  series for modems (\std{V.34}, \std{V.92}), T.\ldots{} for fax
  (\std{T.30}), G.\ldots{} for audio (\std{G.711}) and other series.
\item[\stdb{LAPM}] Link Access Procedure for Modems.  The error
  correction of \std{V.42}.  Most modems use it.  The data goes in
  numbered frames with a checksum.  If a frame has damage, the modem
  sends it again.
\item[Leased line] A permanent connection between two modems, with no
  dialing, no ringing and no dial tone.  Each end starts immediately
  (\cmdl[ampL]{\&L}).  A 2-wire leased line carries the two directions
  on one pair.  A 4-wire line has one pair for each direction.
\item[\stdb{MNP}] Microcom Networking Protocol.  An older family of
  error correction (classes 2 to 4) and compression (class 5), from
  before \std{V.42}.  Many modems still have it (\cmdl[bsN]{\bs N}).
\item[Modem] Modulator-demodulator.  A device that changes the data of a
  computer into sound that can go through a telephone line.  It also
  changes the sound from the far end back into data.  D-Modem does this
  in software, with no modem hardware.
\item[Modulation] The method that the modem uses to change data into
  sound on the line.  The modulation also sets the speed.  Examples are
  \std{V.34}, \std{V.90} and \std{V.92}.  You select the modulation
  with \cmdl[plusMS]{+MS}.
\item[Octet] Eight bits.  This manual writes ``octet'', not ``byte''.
\item[On hook and off hook] The two states of the modem on a telephone
  line.  Off hook, the modem holds the line, as a telephone with its
  handset up.  Then the exchange sends a dial tone, or the call connects.
  On hook, the modem releases the line.  \cmdl[H]{ATH0} puts the modem
  on hook.
\item[\stdb{PCM}] Pulse Code Modulation.  Sound as numbers: the telephone
  network carries a call as 8000 8-bit samples each second (\std{G.711}).
  \std{V.90} and \std{V.92} send data as these numbers.  Thus, they can
  get to \std{56000}~bit/s.
\item[Result code] The answer of the modem to a command line, or
  information about a call: \rc{OK}, \rc{ERROR}, \rc{CONNECT}, \rc{RING},
  \rc{NO CARRIER} and other codes
  (\hyperref[sec:results]{Section~\ref*{sec:results}}).
\item[S-register] A numbered setting.  You read it with
  \cmdr{ATS}\p{n}\cmdr{?} and you set it with
  \cmdr{ATS}\p{n}\cmdr{=}\p{value}
  (\hyperref[sec:sregs]{Section~\ref*{sec:sregs}}).
\item[\stdb{SIP}] Session Initiation Protocol.  The protocol of internet
  telephony.  \texttt{dmodemd} makes and answers its calls as \std{SIP}
  calls (\hyperref[sec:sip]{Section~\ref*{sec:sip}}).
\item[Training and retrain] At the start of a call, each modem sends
  known signals.  Each modem uses the signals from the far end to measure
  the line and to adjust its equalizer and its echo canceller.  This is
  the training.  During a call, the line can change.  Then the modems do the
  training again: this is a retrain.
\item[\stdb{TTY}] Teletypewriter.  A text telephone for persons who are
  deaf or who cannot hear well.  It sends Baudot code at \std{45.45} or
  \std{50}~bit/s (\hyperref[sec:text]{Section~\ref*{sec:text}}).
  On Linux, a tty is also a terminal device in \texttt{/dev}, for
  example a serial port (\texttt{/dev/ttyS0}) or a pseudo terminal
  (\texttt{/dev/pts/3}).  \texttt{dmodemd} is a pseudo terminal.  Thus,
  terminal programs open it as they open a serial modem.
\end{description}

\cmdl[ampA]{\&A\p{n}} & Connection lines before \rc{CONNECT}. & \cmdl[ampB]{\&B\p{n}} & \std{V.32} retrain (accepted). \\ \hline
\cmdl[ampC]{\&C\p{n}} & Data carrier detect (kept). & \cmdl[ampD]{\&D\p{n}} & Data terminal ready (accepted). \\ \hline
\cmdl[ampE]{\&E\p{n}} & Rate in \rc{CONNECT} (kept). & \cmdl[ampF]{\&F\p{n}} & Factory \emph{defaults} (accepted). \\ \hline
